# Execution package

Generated by StackToIT from `execution-plan.json`. Nothing in this folder has been run: StackToIT reads and writes files and creates, changes and deletes nothing in any cloud. Every step is carried out by a person, by OpenTofu or by a script of this folder that a person runs. Paths are relative to the output folder.

Partially executable migration package · Proposal only · Not approved.

**What will be created.** OpenTofu creates 53 resource blocks; the scripts of this package make 4 STACKIT API calls. 11 source resources need no STACKIT resource. Architect work: 272 manual steps in 45 work packages.

**What can run as written.** 48 of the 57 automated steps (OpenTofu or a script) are free of decisions and manual prerequisites (1 waits for a manual prerequisite, 8 require a design decision) · 0 can run as written · 48 of those 48 wait for a value (project_id, carts_service_task_project_roles, carts_task_execution_project_roles, catalog_rds_plan_name and 28 more) · 9 of those 48 rest on a default you have not confirmed (public endpoint, size derived from the source); a person can start 115 of the 272 manual steps now (7 wait for a manual prerequisite, 67 require a design decision, 83 are not executable as the plan stands); 2 steps of the plan are permanently unsupported (refused); 1 step of the plan has its verification incomplete: it carries critical settings of the source that no check validates, which a person confirms in the draft.

**Readiness levels.** 57 automated steps are in the draft · 48 are free of decisions and manual prerequisites · 0 rest on no default to confirm, no opt-in to answer and no value to supply · 0 are approved (every default and decision they rest on is confirmed in the decisions file, and no value they read is left to supply) · 48 of the steps free of decisions and manual prerequisites wait for a value (project_id, carts_service_task_project_roles, carts_task_execution_project_roles, catalog_rds_plan_name and 28 more). 9 automated steps rest on a default nobody confirmed (DEC-GEN-CONVERT-EXPOSURE-WIDENED, DEC-GEN-CONVERT-SIZE-FROM-SOURCE): confirm the default, or choose another option, in decisions.yaml.

**What the figures count.** Three measures, not one: "can run as written" means the step is free of decisions and manual prerequisites and waits for no value (0 of the 57 automated steps); "automation-eligible" means the critical settings of the unit are closed (0 of 44 units; 29 more become eligible once their open items are answered; 15 cannot be automated in this build: a target resource the provider marks experimental (10 units) and 4 more reasons, every one on the Automation eligibility line of index.html); "waiting for a value" means the step is free of decisions and manual prerequisites and reads a variable that has no value yet, so a person supplies it before the step runs and the step is never approved before (48 of the 48 steps free of decisions and manual prerequisites: the plan lists 42 OpenTofu variables, the STACKIT project id among them, with no value).

**What cannot be preserved.** 286 critical settings of 47 source resources that the draft does not carry are open as manual or refused steps ([engineering/not-carried.csv](../engineering/not-carried.csv) lists what the draft does not carry, property by property). 2 steps of the plan are refused: the target has no equivalent (see What cannot be migrated).

**Decisions required.** 9 decisions (17 questions) must be answered before the steps that wait for them can be carried out ([Required decisions](#required-decisions); described in [executive/decisions.md](../executive/decisions.md)). These are the decisions that steps of the plan wait for; the decisions to make of the register (decisions.json) also count the manual actions and plan statements that need a person, so that number can be larger.

**First work package.** [`WP-02`](manual/actions.md), Set up the identities and access (prepare) — owner: Identity Architect, 10 manual steps. The next package that waits, `WP-08` (Write the security group rules the draft does not write (security)), waits for decisions (10 of 13 manual steps). It opens when `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-POD-SEGMENTATION/module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_security_group.this`, `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-POD-SEGMENTATION/module.retail_app_ecs.module.catalog_service.aws_security_group.this`, `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-POD-SEGMENTATION/module.retail_app_ecs.module.checkout_service.aws_security_group.this`, `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-POD-SEGMENTATION/module.retail_app_ecs.module.orders_service.aws_security_group.this`, `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-POD-SEGMENTATION/module.retail_app_ecs.module.ui_service.aws_security_group.this` are answered.

## How the package is carried out

This is an operator-executable migration plan: a person, OpenTofu or a script that a person runs carries out each step, in the order below, and nothing in this folder runs by itself. OpenTofu applies the OpenTofu steps together in one `tofu apply`, so the manual steps and STACKIT API calls that come before it are not enforced: `execution/terraform/apply.md` lists them as a checklist to complete first. An executor that would carry the plan out step by step (`stacktoit execute`) is designed and not implemented.

## Verdict

**Partially executable migration package**

Steps: partially executable: some steps cannot be carried out as the plan stands, whatever is decided: they are marked not executable under Execution order.

The verdict is of the 331 steps of the plan of this package. Every resource and data block of the draft (53) is a step of it. Every source resource of the assessment (52) is a step of it, or needs neither a STACKIT resource nor a step (1). Of them, 11 need no STACKIT resource (`terraform/README.md` lists them under Not needed on STACKIT); 10 of them still have a step of the plan. These files of the draft hold no step of the plan at all: `terraform/terraform.tf`, `terraform/variables.tf`.

Execution completeness: COMPLETE (each of the 272 manual steps states what it is, why, who carries it out and when it is done, and the input it needs where it depends on one).

48 of the 57 automated steps (OpenTofu or a script) are free of decisions and manual prerequisites (1 waits for a manual prerequisite, 8 require a design decision) · 0 can run as written · 48 of those 48 wait for a value (project_id, carts_service_task_project_roles, carts_task_execution_project_roles, catalog_rds_plan_name and 28 more) · 9 of those 48 rest on a default you have not confirmed (public endpoint, size derived from the source); a person can start 115 of the 272 manual steps now (7 wait for a manual prerequisite, 67 require a design decision, 83 are not executable as the plan stands); 2 steps of the plan are permanently unsupported (refused); 1 step of the plan has its verification incomplete: it carries critical settings of the source that no check validates, which a person confirms in the draft. Migration-ready: NO — 286 critical settings of 47 source resources have manual or refused steps open (284 manual, 2 refused); 160 steps of the plan cannot be carried out now (75 wait only for a decision, 83 are not executable whatever is decided, 2 are refused).

The critical settings of the source that the draft does not carry, by source resource: each is a manual or refused step under Execution order (a source resource the draft writes no resource for carries them in its own step); `assurance/critical-semantics.json` says why each is open and what closes it. A setting the knowledge base names by a generated id is said in words before its id: the source attribute it is, as the step of the setting states it, or else its id in words where the plan states no attribute for it.

## What cannot be migrated

- the critical setting `credentials-and-keys-settings` of `module.dependencies.module.catalog_opensearch.aws_opensearch_domain.this`: the target has no counterpart that carries the setting `credentials-and-keys-settings` of aws_opensearch_domain (the knowledge base marks it UNSUPPORTED). The mapping rule for this kind of unit found a setting the target has no counterpart for. The IAM master user, the master user name and password, the customer KMS key and the custom endpoint certificate, read where they are set whatever their value. STACKIT generates the OpenSearch credential (stackit_opensearch_credential username and password are computed), offers no customer KMS key for the instance and no custom endpoint certificate (pinned provider) - UNSUPPORTED; a password is never carried. Reason: the target has no counterpart that carries this setting of the source (`TARGET_SETTING_UNSUPPORTED`).
- the critical setting `domain-credentials-and-keys` of `module.dependencies.module.catalog_opensearch.aws_opensearch_domain.this`: the target has no counterpart that carries the setting `domain-credentials-and-keys` of aws_opensearch_domain (the knowledge base marks it UNSUPPORTED). The mapping rule for this kind of unit found a setting the target has no counterpart for. STACKIT generates the OpenSearch credential (stackit_opensearch_credential username and password are computed), offers no customer KMS key for the instance and no custom endpoint certificate (pinned provider) — UNSUPPORTED. Reason: the target has no counterpart that carries this setting of the source (`TARGET_SETTING_UNSUPPORTED`).

The draft writes no resource for 3 source resources: a person decides how each moves to STACKIT and sets its target up by hand (Manual steps):

- `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_event_rule.ecs_events` — Decided when OpenTofu plans: its count is `(var.container_insights_setting == "enhanced" && var.lifecycle_events_enabled) ? 1 : 0`, which reads 0 as the variables stand, so the draft writes no resource for it. It is not dropped: assess again with a --var-file that sets the input, or decide it after a plan.
- `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_container_insights` — Decided when OpenTofu plans: its count is `(var.container_insights_setting == "enhanced" && var.lifecycle_events_enabled) ? 1 : 0`, which reads 0 as the variables stand, so the draft writes no resource for it. It is not dropped: assess again with a --var-file that sets the input, or decide it after a plan.

Then run `stacktoit assess --decisions FILE` on the same source: an answer frees only the steps its effect allows, and a freed step says which answer freed it.

## Execution order

The steps in the order they are carried out: a step comes after every step it depends on. A step is named here without the name of the generator that made it; `execution-plan.json` holds the full id of each.

A text that many steps share word for word is written once here, with what is the step's own in braces (`{name}` for the name of a resource, `{address}` for an IP address or network, `{steps}` for the steps a state names). The data preflight of module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.aws_elasticache_replication_group.default (`MAN/data/module.dependencies.module.checkout_elasticache_redis.aws_elasticache_replication_group.default/preflight`) — manual step: Close what the plan lists as UNKNOWN, from the move host, with read-only commands; no password goes on a command line or into the history of the shell. Source (`valkey-cli -h \<source-host> -p \<source-port>`): `INFO server` (the engine and its version), `INFO keyspace` and `DBSIZE` (the keys and the keys with an expiry; on every primary when cluster mode is on, listed with `CLUSTER NODES`), `INFO memory` (used_memory and maxmemory), `SCAN 0 COUNT 100` with `TYPE`, `DUMP` and `PTTL` of a few keys of each family (the commands of the copy; the types the copy has no branch for show here), and the credential the copy signs in with. Target (after the draft is applied and the instance and a service key exist; `valkey-cli -h \<target-host> -p \<target-port> --tls --sni \<target-host> --user \<target-user> --askpass`, where `--askpass` makes the client ask for the password of the service key at its prompt): `INFO server` (Valkey 8), `DBSIZE` (0) and `INFO memory`; do not expect `CONFIG`, `ACL` or `MODULE`, which a probe found denied to the service key. Check that the move host is in `parameters.sgw_acl` of the instance (remove it afterwards), reaches both ends, and that the clients of the cache are listed with the TLS and the credential each uses. Runbook section: `terraform/handover/cache-dependencies_checkout_elasticache_redis_default/data-move.md#preflight`. (`terraform/handover/cache-dependencies_checkout_elasticache_redis_default/data-move.md`) After steps 134, 135. (executable: a person can start it now)

Delete the scratch instance afterwards. Runbook section: `terraform/handover/cache-dependencies_checkout_elasticache_redis_default/data-move.md#rehearsal`. After step 136. 193. The data preflight of module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq (`MAN/data/module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq/preflight`) — manual step: Close what the plan lists as UNKNOWN, from the move host, with read-only calls; the passwords go in environment variables, never on a command line that stays in a history. Source (the broker endpoint is the RabbitMQ web console URL of the broker, port 443 or 15671, and the user needs the administrator tag): `curl -u "\<source-user>:$SOURCE_PASSWORD" "\<source-broker-endpoint>/api/overview"` (the RabbitMQ version), `curl -u "\<source-user>:$SOURCE_PASSWORD" "\<source-broker-endpoint>/api/definitions" > definitions-source.json` (the virtual hosts, queues with their types, exchanges, bindings, policies, users and permissions; the file holds password hashes), `curl -u "\<source-user>:$SOURCE_PASSWORD" "\<source-broker-endpoint>/api/queues"` (messages_ready and messages_unacknowledged of each queue, and each queue's type and arguments) and `curl -u "\<source-user>:$SOURCE_PASSWORD" "\<source-broker-endpoint>/api/policies"`; list in the export the streams (Amazon MQ does not support them), the classic mirrored queues and the policies with ha-mode or ha-sync-mode. Target (after the draft is applied and the credential exists; the host and the management URL of the credential stackit_rabbitmq_credential.dependencies_mq are the outputs `dependencies_mq_rabbitmq_credential_host` and `dependencies_mq_rabbitmq_credential_management` of the draft (`tofu output -raw \<name>`), the password is the sensitive output the draft writes): `curl -u "\<target-user>:$TARGET_PASSWORD" "\<target-management-url>/api/overview"`. Check that the move host is in `parameters.sgw_acl` of the instance (remove it afterwards), reaches both management APIs, and that the clients of the broker are listed with the protocol, the library and the credentials each uses. Runbook section: `terraform/handover/broker-dependencies_mq/data-move.md#preflight`. (`terraform/handover/broker-dependencies_mq/data-move.md`) After steps 191, 192. (executable: a person can start it now)
194. The data rehearsal of module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq (`MAN/data/module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq/rehearsal`) — manual step: Rehearse the import on a scratch virtual host of the instance: remove from a copy of the export the users and the permissions (the credential of the instance is the only user, and a hash is not a thing to carry), the policies with ha-mode or ha-sync-mode (classic mirroring is not supported in RabbitMQ 4, `rabbitmqadmin` v2 strips them with strip_cmq_keys_from_policies) and the queues you declare differently on the target (quorum instead of mirrored classic queues); create the virtual host (`curl -u "\<target-user>:$TARGET_PASSWORD" "\<target-management-url>/api/vhosts/rehearsal" -X PUT`), import the copy into it (the entries of the export name the virtual host they belong to, so the copy names the scratch virtual host in them: `curl -u "\<target-user>:$TARGET_PASSWORD" "\<target-management-url>/api/definitions/rehearsal" -X POST -H 'content-type: application/json' -d @definitions-rehearsal.json`), list the result and point one copy of an application at the scratch host to run its tests. Delete the scratch virtual host afterwards (`-X DELETE`). Runbook section: `terraform/handover/broker-dependencies_mq/data-move.md#rehearsal`. After step 193. (executable once its manual prerequisite is done: it waits for step 193, a manual prerequisite)

The import creates structure and moves no message. Runbook section: `terraform/handover/broker-dependencies_mq/data-move.md#initial-copy-definitions-and-drain`. After step 194. Record GO or NO-GO. The drain of the queues and the switch of the producers and the consumers follow as the steps of the cutover. The target instance must be empty before the load (the STACKIT how-to of the restore says so): load the dump with the mysql client, as the user of the instance's service key, into the empty instance: `mysql --host=\<target-host> --port=\<target-port> --user=\<target-user> --password --database=\<target-database> < \<dump-file>.sql 2> load.log`, and read every line of `load.log`. A DEFINER account that does not exist on the target, a collation the target does not know and a database the target does not have are the errors the checks name: each is settled by a person, never by skipping the object. The drain of the queue of module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq (`MAN/cutover/messaging-drain/module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq`) — manual step: Drain the queues of the AWS broker module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq (aws_mq_broker): its consumers go on reading them, with no producer sending to the broker any more, until every queue holds no message. Read the depth in CloudWatch: Amazon MQ for RabbitMQ publishes MessageCount (the sum of the ready and the unacknowledged messages) for the broker and, on 3.x brokers, for each queue (the dimensions Queue and VirtualHost; on 4.x brokers the figures of a queue come from the Prometheus endpoint), and Amazon MQ for ActiveMQ publishes QueueSize and InFlightCount for each queue and TotalMessageCount for the broker. On a RabbitMQ broker the management API gives the same figure for every queue: `curl -s -u "\<source-user>:$SOURCE_PASSWORD" "\<source-broker-endpoint>/api/queues" | jq '[.[] | (.messages_ready // 0) + (.messages_unacknowledged // 0)] | add'` is 0 when they are empty. An inactive durable topic subscriber (InactiveDurableTopicSubscribersCount) holds messages that only its own client can read: connect it to the AWS broker until it has read them. The plan does not read the names of the queues of the broker. After step 244. ## Terraform commands

`execution/terraform/apply.md` lists every OpenTofu step with its resource and file. OpenTofu applies the whole draft in one run and enforces none of the manual steps, STACKIT API calls or phases of the plan: complete the steps that `execution/terraform/apply.md` lists under "Complete these steps before tofu apply" first. 4 OpenTofu steps are not executable yet: a full apply creates every block of the draft as it stands, theirs included, so apply only when every OpenTofu step is executable. From the output folder:

```sh
cd terraform
tofu init
tofu validate
tofu plan -out=stackit.tfplan
tofu apply stackit.tfplan
```

## API commands

Each call has a request file (the call as data) and a script that renders it. A script is run with `bash <script>` from any folder; it stops before the call when its step is not executable, when an input is not set, or when the call needs a confirmation.

The execution order names, as "Step state", how far the step of a call has come: "generated, not yet tested offline" (a converter wrote it and no test ran its script against a local stub), "generated, tested offline" (a test of StackToIT ran the script under a real shell against a local stub), "verified live" (a recorded probe against the STACKIT API shows the step does what it states) and "catalogued, not generated" (the call is known and described and no converter writes it). StackToIT runs none of these calls itself.

A script compares the answer of its call with the answer the plan expects (the HTTP status exactly, and a member when the plan names one). It stops with exit 3 when its step is not executable, 4 when the call needs the confirmation, 5 when an input, a tool or a file is missing or unsafe, 6 when its lookup cannot tell whether the object exists, 7 when the answer is not the expected one, and 8 when a postcondition it checks does not hold.

A script that calls a STACKIT API with curl trusts the certificate authorities curl trusts. For an API behind a private authority, set `STACKIT_CA_BUNDLE` to a PEM file of its certificates (curl's --cacert); the certificate is always verified. Each call ends after `STACKIT_CALL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` seconds (120 unless it names others, at most 3600), so an API that does not answer stops the script with a non-zero exit instead of holding it; a call that timed out may still have been made, so read the target before running the script again.

A script that writes a secret into STACKIT Secrets Manager (the Vault-compatible API) logs in as the Secrets Manager user the draft creates, with the username and password that user has once the draft is applied (the script reads them from the draft's outputs with tofu output, or from its `STACKIT_INPUT_USERNAME` and `STACKIT_INPUT_PASSWORD` variables), and writes the document of key/value pairs that the file named in its `STACKIT_INPUT_SECRET_DOCUMENT_<n>` variable holds. The document is a file of yours: the package holds no value of any secret, and the script prints no value, puts none on a command line and keeps none outside its own temporary folder. A secret that already has a current version stops the script (exit 4) until `STACKIT_CONFIRM_REPLACE` is set to the path of the secret, because the write makes a new version the current one. The user is a migration user with write access to every secret of the instance and its password is in the OpenTofu state: delete the user (remove its block from the draft and apply) once the secrets are written.

## Manual steps

272 steps are carried out by a person, each described in `execution/manual/actions.md`: what, why, who, when, what it needs and when it is done. Roles named: Data Architect, Identity Architect, Migration Owner, Network Architect, Platform Architect, Security Architect, Storage Architect.

## Verification

`execution/verify/checks.md` lists 299 checks to hold before the steps and 70 checks to hold after them, one checklist row each. 1 of them confirms a critical setting the draft carries (verification only): a person confirms it in the file of the draft, and no step sets it up by hand.

## Rollback

Undoing a step: 68 can be undone (the step says how), 10 only in part (what was written since is lost), 248 by a person, 5 cannot be undone. From the cutover on, steps 203, 204, 205, 206, 207, 208, 209, 210, 211, 212, 213, 214, 215, 216, 217, 218, 219, 220, 221, 222, 223, 224, 225, 226, 227, 228, 229, 230, 231, 232, 233, 234, 236, 237, 238, 239, 240, 241, 242, 243, 244, 245, 246, 247, 248, 249, 250, 251, 252, 253, 254, 255, 256, 257, 258, 274, 275, 276, 277, 278, 279, 280, 281, 282, 283, 284, 285, 286, 287, 288, 289, 290, 291, 292, 293, 294, 295, 296, 297, 298, 299, 300, 301, 302, 303, 304, 305, 306, 307, 308, 309, 310, 311, 312, 313, 314, 315, 316, 317, 318, 319, 320, 321, 322, 323, 324, 325, 326, 327, 328, 329, 330, 331 cannot be undone in full by a tool: decide how before the cutover starts. Each step's rollback is in the actions sheet and, for a manual step, in `execution/manual/actions.md`.

## Files of this package

- `execution-plan.json` — the plan itself, machine-readable, at the root of the run: the one file of the plan (the package holds no copy of it)
- `execution/terraform/apply.md` — the OpenTofu steps and commands
- `execution/api/` — one request file and one script for each STACKIT API call
- `execution/manual/actions.md` — the manual steps and what is not migrated
- `execution/verify/checks.md` — the checks before and after each step
- `migration/execution-actions.csv` — one row per step, for a spreadsheet
- `migration/work-packages.csv` — one row per work package of the manual steps, for a spreadsheet
